that hypothesis or claim a full tensor reconstruction from commutation alone.

### Complete finite Lorentz orbit coordinates (26 October 2026)

This slice formalizes the corresponding source-A coordinates Q_x = Θ_x η Θ_xᵀ
and K_(x,r) = Θ_x L_(x,r) Θ_(x+r)⁻¹ from §A of the pinned nonlinear J²
bridge. The source pin is commit `77d3ab6059bd1a3ce48637b97b9f30aa8825fc5f`,

`ce56193edac787640840921310d265e5ebb5a0bd4d628414109dacaa6836b7bc`. On the finite raw-solder carrier, `D0.Geometry.A4DStarFiniteLorentzQuotient` formalizes the coordinate-completeness statement as an application candidate. At one site,
`a4dRightLorentz_gram_complete` shows that equality of the Gram matrices is
equivalent to a right action by `IsRoleLorentz`, provided the reference solder
matrix is invertible. `a4dDressedLink_eq_iff_rightAction` proves that equality
of the dressed links then forces exactly the corresponding endpoint action on
the undressed link. The field theorem `a4d_complete_orbit_coordinates`
combines these statements: on an all-site nondegenerate reference coframe,
equality of every site Gram and every dressed link is equivalent to one
site-dependent Lorentz field relating both the complete coframe and link
fields. Its signature retains the all-site determinant-unit premise. The link
field carrier is arbitrary matrix-valued data; no hidden link-group premise is
used.

The Lean predicate `IsRoleLorentz` is exactly
Λ * η * Λ.transpose = η, so this theorem covers the full orthogonal group
O(1,3). It does not encode the discrete orientation and
time-orientation labels needed to restrict the result to a fixed
SO⁺(1,3) component. Nor does it prove that the source's selected finite
action descends to this Lean carrier: the action-to-carrier map and its
independent semantic review remain open.

### Proper determinant-component refinement (26 October 2026)

The finite quotient module now records the relative determinant-orientation
condition explicitly and proves a second orbit-completeness result. For
nondegenerate reference solder frames, equal site Grams plus
`0 < det(Theta) * det(Psi)` give a Lorentz witness with determinant one. At
field level, equality of every site Gram and dressed link, together with that
orientation condition at every site, is equivalent to one site-dependent
determinant-one frame action relating the coframe and link fields. The
definition `IsProperRoleLorentz` means `IsRoleLorentz` plus `det = 1`; it does
not impose time orientation, so the `SO+(1,3)` restriction remains open.

### Shared finite-Gram orbit API and Lorentz specialization (26 October 2026)

The finite Lorentz Gram-completeness proof repeated matrix algebra already
owned by the generic finite-Gram module. That argument is now factored into
`D0.Algebra.finiteGram_right_orbit_complete` in
`D0.Algebra.FiniteGramTangent`. For any finite index type and real matrix
form `η`, it proves

`finiteGram η Θ = finiteGram η Ψ ↔ ∃ Λ, finiteGram η Λ = η ∧ Ψ = Θ * Λ`

from invertibility of the reference frame `Θ`. The fixed form `η` need not be
invertible. The converse reuses `finiteGram_right_invariant`; the forward
direction constructs the frame witness `Θ⁻¹ * Ψ` and proves its isometry
equation from Gram equality.

`D0.Geometry.a4dRightLorentz_gram_complete` now specializes this common API to
`roleLorentzMetric` and the existing `IsRoleLorentz` predicate. The compiled
proof dependencies confirm the specialization calls the generic theorem, and
the generic theorem calls `finiteGram_right_invariant`. The site-field and
proper-determinant results continue through that specialization, preserving
their existing nondegeneracy and relative-orientation hypotheses.

The Lean files are pinned at commit `b1e59692ac222c0bf564f9d6a97e3d2ca64aa4f2`:
`FiniteGramTangent.lean` SHA-256
`ece56ab3725d39a7388278b886b68480508db02ed1d828a56e11ddaa12a70bef`, and
`A4DStarFiniteLorentzQuotient.lean` SHA-256
`136101ff7acec2be8be853c8b64a8ce2c1b31ab036e9c931c195554ad93610d6`. Their
combined Lean-source digest is
`dabf250dc4288c227cfc51c8f6b2352dae221b14717d78c8b08d705445db19de`.
The algebra module built at **2,946/2,946** jobs, the dependent geometry module
at **3,215/3,215**, and the final public `D0.All` integration at
**4,581/4,581**. The two theorem declarations retain the standard

The result is finite matrix orbit algebra and a typed Lean reuse edge. It does

invertibility only for the Lorentz frame changes. That premise is now removed
from the adapter and its complete-orbit application. This narrows the theorem
to the actual algebraic hypotheses. The converse equality characterization
still needs both endpoint-frame determinant units, and endpoint metric
compatibility still needs the transported-to frame to be nondegenerate.

### Exact affine finite-Gram expansion (26 October 2026)

`D0.Algebra.finiteGram_add_smul_expand` now gives the exact finite quadratic
expansion at an arbitrary baseline matrix:

`finiteGram η (Θ + t • C) = finiteGram η Θ +
 t • (C * η * Θ.transpose + Θ * η * C.transpose) +
 t^2 • (C * η * C.transpose)`.

The statement retains both first-order sides and the quadratic remainder. The
prior `finiteGram_smul_expand` and `finiteGram_one_smul_expand` declarations
remain as specializations and their compiled proof dependencies now point to
the shared theorem. The typed consumer inventory also records
`solderMetric_smul_expand` and `j2DetectorMetricPath_smul_expand` using those
specializations, followed by their corresponding symmetric-tangent application
theorems. The source maps therefore retain the complete linear and quadratic
terms instead of duplicating the algebraic expansion.

### Shared exact finite-Gram difference quotient (26 October 2026)

`D0.Algebra.finiteGram_add_smul_difference_quotient` now derives the exact
normalized difference from the arbitrary-baseline expansion, for `t ≠ 0`:

`t⁻¹ • (finiteGram η (Θ + t • C) - finiteGram η Θ) =
 (C * η * Θ.transpose + Θ * η * C.transpose) +
 t • (C * η * C.transpose)`.

The complete two-sided linear source and the finite-parameter quadratic
remainder are retained. `solderMetric_flat_tangent` and
`j2DetectorMetricPath_symmetric_difference_quotient` now consume this generic
quotient directly; their prior hand-written cancellation and scalar
simplification proofs are removed. The solder adapter separately proves its
flat baseline identity `finiteGram η η = η` from the declared Lorentz metric
transpose and involution facts. This is an exact finite difference identity,
not a continuum derivative or physical field equation.

### Generic finite-Gram Fréchet derivative and J² consumer (26 October 2026)

The finite Gram API now has a separate calculus layer in
`D0.Algebra.FiniteGramDerivative`. For every finite square real-matrix carrier,
with the Frobenius norm and no symmetry or invertibility assumption on `η` or
`Θ`, `finiteGram_hasFDerivAt` proves the full derivative of
`X ↦ X η Xᵀ` at every frame `Θ`. Its continuous-linear derivative has the
explicit two-sided action
`H ↦ H η Θᵀ + Θ η Hᵀ`. The module also owns the reusable Frobenius transpose
isometry and `finiteGramTangent_apply`.

`D0.Geometry.A4DSolderMetricCompletion` now specializes that API for the
pinned J² flat-frame chart `H ↦ finiteGram η (1 + H)`: its old
`j2DetectorMetricPath_tangent` is a compatibility name for the generic
tangent, and `j2DetectorMetricPath_hasFDerivAt_flat` follows by composing the
generic derivative with `H ↦ 1 + H`. This removes the repeated local product
and transpose derivative proof while preserving public geometry declarations.
The existing sitewise solder-map and complete finite-field derivative continue
to consume the same J² result. The API remains finite matrix calculus; it does
not supply the selected-action-to-carrier map, physical source choice,
continuum variation, or GR closure.

The narrow build passed for `D0.Algebra.FiniteGramDerivative`
(**3,053/3,053 jobs**) and `D0.Geometry.A4DSolderMetricCompletion`
(**3,088/3,088 jobs**). The compiled public-`D0.All` metric-provenance
crosswalk enumerates both modules and resolves all five new algebra locators
plus `j2DetectorMetricPath_hasFDerivAt_flat`. The declaration inventory reports
`Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`, and `propext` as the trust footprint; the
source adds no project axiom or admitted proof. Its source digest is
`102620bb64320e24feea3247ab2aec3e4787ab4e039d8aec6e25669d97b0e309`; the
clean crosswalk snapshot at `aaae64c2fb815e29bdee97c0768e34be88343841` has

inverse-coframe-matrix variable is `H ↦ H * (Λ.transpose)⁻¹`, with the Lorentz
link and endpoint maps held fixed. The proof composes

derivative of right multiplication.

### Eq. (26) endpoint blindness and non-null Eq. (27) separation (26 October 2026)

The endpoint-map application now distinguishes what the two pinned Julia
readouts can establish. `archiveJuliaSourceCARRaisingQuery_nonNull_endpoint_map_injective`
reuses the existing Eq. (27) recovery map: for a common source vector with
nonzero Lorentz norm, two source-form-preserving, determinant-one linear
equivalences that give the same complete CAR raising query must be equal.
This is a direct composition of the owned endpoint decoder, not a second
derivation of its formula.

The concrete countercontrol uses the diagonal A/B sign flip
`archiveJuliaABSignFlip`. Its determinant is one, it preserves the source
Lorentz form, and its induced endpoint equivalence differs from identity.
Nevertheless, the assembled Eq. (26) vacuum-column matrix is identical for
the identity and sign-flip endpoint families. For the non-null A basis source,
the complete CAR query separates that same pair. The controlled result shows
why vacuum-column equality alone cannot be used as the 24-link inverse; it
does not compute the link derivative or the full `D R D Φ = I`.

### Chart-complete pathwise derivative recovery for endpoint links (26 October 2026)

The next link-side application is now compiled in
`D0.Geometry.A4DJuliaLinkPathDifferential`. Its canonical
`principalFrameJuliaEndpointChartReadout` chooses the owned Eq. (27) decoder
off the null locus and the observed-query Eq. (28) decoder on the null locus.
`principalFrameJuliaEndpointChartReadout_eq` proves that this selected
readout returns each actual role endpoint `M r` for the same constructed
Julia query family, using the existing two-chart coverage theorem. It keeps
the source-form isometry, determinant-one, normalized covector, and nonzero
shift assumptions explicit; on the null branch, the existing coverage proof
derives the projected-covector and positive-branch conditions.

`principalFrameJuliaEndpointChartReadout_hasDerivAt_of_properRoleLorentzPath`
then considers any differentiable path of four role-indexed proper-Lorentz
endpoint matrices, with the coframe fixed and each link allowed to vary
independently. For every role and input vector, when the path remains
Lorentz, determinant one, and inside the same nonzero-shift charts, the
query-to-readout path has exactly the derivative of that endpoint acting on
the vector. The proof uses the same concrete CAR query and chart-selected
decoder at each path parameter, covering both non-null Eq. (27) and nonzero
null Eq. (28) cases.

### Exponential integration of joint Lorentz tangent generators (26 October 2026)

The joint path result now has a tangent-generated specialization in
`D0.Geometry.A4DJuliaLinkPathDifferential`:
`principalFrameJuliaEndpointChartReadout_hasDerivAt_of_jointCoframeAndLorentzTangent`.
For any four role-indexed matrices satisfying the existing
`IsRoleLorentzTangent` equations, it constructs the simultaneous paths
`Λ(t,r) = exp(t • a(r))`. The path proof uses the tangent identity to show
`Λ η Λᵀ = η`. Taking determinants gives `det(Λ)^2 = 1`; continuity of the
matrix exponential and determinant, together with `det(Λ(0,r)) = 1`, rules
out the negative branch for every real `t`.

The endpoint derivative is proved entrywise from the matrix-exponential
derivative and the finite sum defining `Λ(t,r)ᵀ.mulVec x`. The theorem then
feeds these constructed Lorentz and determinant-one paths into the existing
joint coframe/link composition. Coframe `F`, column data `L`, and normalized
covectors may still vary with `t`, with the same pointwise normalization and
nonzero-shift chart conditions. The derivative at the identity is exactly
`a(r)ᵀ.mulVec x`.

This removes the standalone path-existence assumption for tangent generators
in the identity-connected exponential directions. It does not construct the
full six-coordinate Lorentz chart or show that every determinant-one Lorentz
link lies on such a path. The 24-direction joint `D R D Φ = I`, physical
endpoint/query admission, full price/source selection, #334 acceptance,
`GR_closed`, and independent C0 acceptance remain open.

### Full joint Fréchet differential on the Julia Lorentz tangent product (26 October 2026)

`D0.Geometry.A4DJuliaLinkPathDifferential` now packages the four
`IsRoleLorentzTangent` spaces as the product parameter space and defines the
actual chart-selected Julia endpoint readout along their matrix-exponential
links. `principalFrameJuliaEndpointChartReadoutAlongLorentzTangent_hasFDerivAt`
proves one Fréchet derivative for the complete four-role output at the
identity parameter. Its continuous linear derivative is exposed as
`roleLorentzTangentEndpointFDeriv`; the companion
`roleLorentzTangentEndpointFDeriv_apply` states its exact action at each role:
the role's tangent generator, transposed, applied to the recovered endpoint
vector. The proof factors through the existing exact Eq. (27)/(28) chart
recovery and the Fréchet derivative of the matrix exponential at zero.

This consumes the prior pathwise derivative result and removes the need to
assume a separate one-parameter path for each directional derivative. Its
domain is precisely the product of the four declared Lorentz tangent
subspaces, with fixed coframe, column data, and normalized covectors satisfying
the existing chart hypotheses. It does not prove that the tangent subspace has
dimension six, give a six-coordinate chart for the whole group, cover links
outside the identity exponential image, supply the full source's variational
pullback (31), or select physical endpoint admission or price/source. GR
closure and independent C0 acceptance remain open.

### Canonical six-coordinate Lorentz tangent reused by the Julia differential (26 October 2026)

The product-domain caveat from the preceding Fréchet result is now closed at
the level of tangent dimension. `A4DJuliaLinkPathDifferential` no longer
constructs a second copy of the Lorentz tangent submodule: its existing
`roleLorentzTangentSubmodule` name is a compatibility alias for the canonical
`RoleLorentzTangent` in `A4DSolderMetricCompletion`. That owner supplies the
actual coordinate equivalence
`rolePairsEquivLorentzTangent : (RolePair → ℝ) ≃ₗ[ℝ] RoleLorentzTangent`
and the compiled theorem `roleLorentzTangent_finrank : finrank = 6`.

The Julia consumer now proves
`roleLorentzTangentParameters_finrank : Module.finrank ℝ
RoleLorentzTangentParameters = 24`, by taking the finite product of the four
canonical six-dimensional role spaces. Thus the prior Fréchet differential's
domain is exactly a 24-dimensional real tangent parameter space, and its
rolewise derivative formula remains unchanged. This is a type-preserving
refactor and application of the shared coordinate API; it does not supply a
six-coordinate chart for the full Lorentz group, make the exponential map
surjective onto every determinant-one link, prove the source's full
variational pullback (31), or select physical endpoint admission or
price/source. Physical GR and independent C0 acceptance remain open.

### Explicit canonical coordinates for the Julia endpoint differential (26 October 2026)

The next application now consumes the shared six-coordinate Lorentz tangent
equivalence directly. `roleLorentzTangentParametersEquivCoordinates` is a
linear equivalence from the four-role tangent parameter to
`Role → RolePair → ℝ`; its inverse reconstructs each role's tangent generator
with `rolePairsEquivLorentzTangent`. The new continuous-linear map
`roleLorentzTangentEndpointFDerivInCoordinates` gives the Julia endpoint
Fréchet differential in those explicit 24 real coordinates. Its pointwise
theorem states that the output at role `r` is the transpose action on `x` of
the tangent reconstructed from that role's six `RolePair` coordinates.

### Exact cubic-time midpoint recovery on the same positive nonvacuum carrier (25 October 2026)

This continues the pinned positive nonvacuum source slice at
[A4D_EXACT_NONVACUUM_GR_MIDPOINT_C2_RECOVERY_2026_10_20.md](https://github.com/gvakhrushev/d0_15/blob/8b94198c8dfbdf5983ad0b350b058fb30bb68a54/02_REGISTRY/research/A4D_EXACT_NONVACUUM_GR_MIDPOINT_C2_RECOVERY_2026_10_20.md),
commit `8b94198c8dfbdf5983ad0b350b058fb30bb68a54`, blob
`63c36b18af67fc1f113f9e33deb13cf43ddf4556`, §§7.1–7.2. It adds a real
finite-mesh application within the existing owner; it creates no independent
claim row or scientific task.

`D0.Geometry.NonvacuumBrinkmannStencil` now defines a two-component cubic
profile, its exact time derivative, and the actual chronological record
formed by applying the existing midpoint update at every interval. For each
component and every natural number of steps, Lean proves the exact identity

`psi_h[n,i] = psi_i(n*h) - c3[i]*n*h^3/4`.

Taking absolute values gives the exact remainder
`(n*|h|)*|6*c3[i]|*|h|^2/24`. Therefore, if `M` bounds
`|6*c3[i]| = |psi_i'''|` for both components, the accumulated record obeys
the componentwise bound

`|psi_h[n,i] - psi_i(n*h)| <= (n*|h|)*M*|h|^2/24`.

This is a formal cubic-time specialization of the midpoint recovery estimate,
with no positive-mesh assumption needed for the absolute-value statement; it
does not establish the source’s estimate for arbitrary smooth profiles.

The concrete application theorem
`D0.Geometry.nonvacuumCubicMidpointSource_matches_record` evaluates the
five-point spatial source and matter derivative at the same time midpoint.
It proves that this source equals `-4 * coupling` times the norm of the
forward difference of the same recursively built record at consecutive steps. Its
only mesh premise is `h ≠ 0`; the four spatial profile coefficient functions
are arbitrary at that midpoint. The proof specializes
`nonvacuumProfile_source_eq_finiteMidpointMatterNorm`, retaining its actual
application dependency and hypotheses.

The first cubic checkpoint recorded a 34-declaration nonvacuum module at
source digest `03da778902f4eb3c2c96219f0618213ac7c5670ac8327b6f611f091f4f37d3e4`.
Those inventories were superseded when the shared finite partial-trace module
was integrated. The current combined public-root inventories are recorded in
the following checkpoint. The earlier 20/20 cold-start run covered the cubic
queries before that integration; the combined runner is replayed separately.
This closes one explicit mathematical and application slice only. The
arbitrary-smooth-field refinement theorem, original CAR construction,
40-to-3-to-1 substitution, all 16+24+2 Euler equations, independent C0
freeze/acceptance, and physical Book/clock/source/execution selection remain
open under their existing owners. No physical status changes.

### Local 40-coordinate Julia readout at arbitrary proper base links (26 October 2026)

The Julia endpoint differential now applies at any supplied rolewise
determinant-one Lorentz link. The family
`roleLorentzTangentProperFamilyAtBase Λ₀ hDet₀ q` is the right translation
`Λ₀ r * exp (rolePairsEquivLorentzTangent (q r))`; `hΛ₀` retains the
rolewise Lorentz-isometry hypotheses and `hDet₀` retains determinant one.
The theorem
`principalFrameJuliaJointCoframeEndpointReadoutAtBaseInCoordinates_hasFDerivAt`
proves the Fréchet derivative at `(F₀, 0)` for the 16 coframe matrix entries
and 24 canonical tangent coordinates `Role → RolePair → ℝ`. Its output is
the coframe variation paired with the endpoint derivative of the
right-translated Lorentz action on `x`.

The proof composes the exact Eq. (30a) inverse-coframe recovery with the
Eq. (27)/(28) endpoint readout identity over one Julia query family. It uses
local normalized-covector and nonzero-shift chart conditions for every coframe
in a positive-radius neighborhood. The covector field itself need not be
differentiable: local equality of the exact readout with the Lorentz-action
model supplies the derivative. `RolePair` coordinates reuse the existing
six-dimensional Lorentz tangent API at each of four roles.

### Exact time-indexed midpoint source on the nonvacuum stencil (26 October 2026)

The existing finite nonvacuum fragment now extends from one arbitrary
two-component midpoint step to a chronological rate sequence
`rate : ℕ → Fin 2 → ℝ`. The recursive `finiteMatterMidpointHistory` now uses
the canonical `finiteMatterMidpointStep` at every successor. Its compiled
telescoping theorem `finiteMatterMidpointHistory_eq_base_add_rateSum` proves
the exact endpoint law

```text
Ψ_n = Ψ_0 + h * ∑_{k < n} rate_k.
```

The compiled theorem `twoComponentMatterHistorySquaredGradientSum_eq_rateEnergySum`
also proves the finite-horizon identity

```text
∑_{k < n} ||(Ψ_(k+1) - Ψ_k)/h||² = ∑_{k < n} ||rate_k||²,
```

for `h ≠ 0`, by composing the per-step history theorem. It identifies the
actual accumulated record-gradient energy with the same sequence of rate
energies; it does not identify a physical Cost/Info price.

The profile `finiteTimeNonvacuumBrinkmannProfile` uses that same step rate's
squared norm at each site. The compiled theorem
`finiteTimeNonvacuumProfile_source_eq_midpointHistoryNorm` composes the exact
five-point profile equation with the actual adjacent record values:

```text
Δ_h^⊥ H_n(x,y)
  = -4 coupling * ||(Ψ_(n+1) - Ψ_n)/h||²
```

for every `h ≠ 0`, every `n`, arbitrary two-component rate and base record,
and arbitrary time-dependent quadratic/cubic harmonic coefficients. This is a
finite discrete same-history source identity; it assumes no smooth rate law
and proves no continuum convergence estimate. It makes explicit that the
sitewise profile source and the history gradient use the same rate rather than
an independently supplied energy value.

The merged module also retains the explicit cubic matter-profile application:
`cubicMatterMidpointSample_eq_profile_sub_cubic_error` gives the exact
accumulated midpoint error, `cubicMatterMidpointSample_error_bound` bounds it
by `N |h|^3 M / 24`, and `nonvacuumCubicMidpointSource_matches_record` ties
that same midpoint rate to the Brinkmann source. This bound applies to the
stated cubic profile family; it is not the general smooth-field recovery
estimate.

`D0.All` build passed **4,585/4,585** jobs with Lean 4.30.0. The Lean-source
digest is `6c73bc54dfdcbe972a58bdd615c173c2bf8cf051a3c394a57120a2112e664644`.

`33d522c7340f186f551d2560ec694833548068c528523378d3503b5558c1d21a`; they

### Composed midpoint refinement error from local defects (26 October 2026)

The chronological matter history now has an exact componentwise error
decomposition against an arbitrary exact sample sequence. For each component,
the history error at node `n` equals the sum of the signed local defects

```text
h * rate_k - (Psi_(k+1) - Psi_k),  k < n.
```

The compiled Lean theorem `finiteMatterMidpointHistory_error_eq_localDefectSum`
proves this identity without smoothness assumptions. Its absolute-value
corollaries show that the accumulated error is bounded by the sum of local
absolute defects. If each component's per-cell defect is at most
`M * h^3 / 24`, `h ≥ 0`, `M ≥ 0`, and the interval length is `N * h`, then
`finiteMatterMidpointHistory_error_le_refinementBound` proves for both
components and every node `n ≤ N` the uniform bound

```text
|Psi_h(n) - Psi(n)| ≤ |I| * M * h^2 / 24.
```

This formalizes the finite accumulation step in the source's Eq. (24). The
rate sequence is still an explicit input, and the local `M * h^3 / 24` bound
is a hypothesis: the Lean module does not yet derive it from the source's
`C^3` regularity statement. The result is componentwise; no equal-constant
Euclidean vector norm estimate is claimed. It also does not select the native
CAR preparation, the original 40→3→1 Cartan variation, the 16+24+2 Euler
rows, or the physical Book/clock/source.

The changed module passes the narrow build (**2,946/2,946** jobs) and the
public-import `D0.All` build (**4,585/4,585** jobs) with Lean 4.30.0. Exact
source snapshot `d27cafffe9592416556c9f5eb4bd069a355dca44` is clean; the module
digest is `369d1159021853c3ec8726a1b9c32518dfb288a0238fdd43440f31df7c4b33e5`.
The compiled `D0.All` inventory records 39 declarations owned by the module,
including generated auxiliaries; all five new source declarations have the
same standard axiom footprint `{Classical.choice, Quot.sound, propext}`.

(SHA-256 `2a3838ff0055d054e3e584254489b62f23c3a26079236f99d94ad473a9549a6f`).

### Historical C^3 midpoint defect before shared API extraction (26 October 2026)

The remaining analytic hypothesis in the composed midpoint refinement is now
proved for exact samples of the same two component functions. The source input
is the chronological midpoint construction in the pinned positive case,
`A4D_EXACT_NONVACUUM_GR_MIDPOINT_C2_RECOVERY_2026_10_20.md`, §§7.1–7.2,
commit `8b94198c8dfbdf5983ad0b350b058fb30bb68a54`, blob
`63c36b18af67fc1f113f9e33deb13cf43ddf4556`.

`midpointQuadrature_error_abs_le_of_thirdDerivative_bound` applies the
Lagrange remainder at the cell midpoint. The quadratic Taylor terms cancel
between the two endpoints, and each endpoint remainder is at most
`M * h^3 / 48`; their sum gives the local `M * h^3 / 24` defect. The theorem
assumes `ContDiff ℝ 3 f` and an explicit bound on `|iteratedDeriv 3 f|` over
that cell. `sampledMatterMidpointLocalDefect_abs_le` applies it to each of the
two component functions with midpoint derivative rates and exact endpoint
samples. `sampledMatterMidpointHistory_error_le_refinementBound` then composes
those local bounds with the previously proved history-error identity, yielding
for every component and node `n ≤ N`:

```text
|Psi_h(n) - Psi(n)| ≤ duration * M * h^2 / 24.
```

This supplies the classical mathematical refinement estimate for the stated
sampled functions and a common third-derivative bound. It does not derive that
bound from the CAR operator, select a physical matter source or clock, identify
the sampled trajectory with an admitted D0 execution, formalize the original
40→3→1 Cartan variation, or close the independent 16+24+2 Euler equations.
The estimate is componentwise; no same-constant Euclidean vector norm claim is
made. It remains a conditional mathematics input to the positive nonvacuum
example, not physical source selection or GR closure.

### Shared midpoint quadrature API refactor (26 October 2026)

The scalar Taylor/Lagrange estimate has been moved out of the nonvacuum
geometry module into the reusable `D0.Analysis.MidpointQuadrature` module.
Its theorem
`D0.Analysis.midpointQuadrature_error_abs_le_of_thirdDerivative_bound`
retains the same statement: for a scalar `C^3` function with third derivative
bounded by `M` on the cell, the midpoint increment defect is at most
`M * h^3 / 24`. The quadratic terms still cancel at the midpoint, and the two
endpoint remainders still contribute `M * h^3 / 48` each.

`D0.Geometry.NonvacuumBrinkmannStencil` now contains the concrete two-component
application. `sampledMatterMidpointLocalDefect_abs_le` applies the shared
calculus theorem to each exact sampled component, and the sampled-history
theorem composes that result with the existing finite accumulation bound.

### Fixed continuum profile at the half-step coframe readout (26 October 2026)

The follow-up formalizes the site map left open above. The source convention
assigns coframe site `j` the readout
`τⱼ = x₀ + j h - h/2`; the midpoint of cell `n` is exactly `τ_(n+1)`. The
Lean theorem `sampledNonvacuumBrinkmannProfile_eq_fixedReadout` identifies the
finite profile with the original fixed continuum profile sampled at that
readout, with each of the four harmonic coefficients sampled at the same
`τ_(n+1)`. The theorem
`sampledFixedNonvacuumBrinkmannProfile_source_eq_siteMatter` then proves that
the fixed-profile five-point stencil at this site equals the matter source
`-4 coupling · ‖ψ'(τ_(n+1))‖²`. These are exact scalar algebraic identities
for arbitrary functions and nonzero `h`.

### Componentwise sufficient condition for positive CAR complement (26 October 2026)

The exact owner statement remains pinned to #334 commit
`fe5cf335da04cac14e50407b03f98b96be17dc52`, file blob
`67531a343a0ae707ff0a3830c0e2c9cf2bbc8213`, §§7.1–7.2, Eq. (24). That
equation controls the Euclidean norm of the two-component midpoint-history
error and uses the margin
`δ = sqrt(1-p) - sup_I ‖Ψ‖ > 0`, with the sufficient threshold
`|I| M h^2 / 24 < δ/2`.

The new Lean application is deliberately componentwise. From the existing
coordinatewise estimate, it assumes each continuum component is bounded by
`A`, each component third derivative by `M`, `N h = duration` with `h ≥ 0`,
and the direct slack condition
`2 * (A + duration * M * h^2 / 24)^2 < 1-p`. It proves that every finite
history value through index `N` has a strictly positive complementary
amplitude `sqrt(1-p-‖Ψ_h‖²)`. The proof bounds each discrete component by its
continuum sample plus the scalar midpoint error, sums the two squared bounds,
and applies positivity of the square root.

The compiled declarations are
`D0.Geometry.twoComponentMatterNormSq_le_two_mul_sq_of_abs_le` and
`D0.Geometry.sampledMatterMidpointCARComplement_positive_of_componentwiseSlack`,
with the corresponding radicand/amplitude definitions. This is a concrete
componentwise sufficient condition; it is not the source's vector-norm
estimate or its `δ/2` threshold. The present hypotheses still assume global
C³ component functions and an explicit third-derivative bound; the source's
interval smoothness statement and the bound's derivation from the actual CAR
generator are not formalized here.

An independent read-only scope review returned **scoped PASS** for the
implication under these stated hypotheses: the coordinate error, continuum
envelope and squared-norm bound imply a positive radicand at each `n ≤ N`.
The reviewer confirmed that this does not prove Eq. (24)'s vector estimate,
the `δ/2` threshold, or validity of the component bounds for the actual CAR
preparation, and did not run a build. The review accepts only this conditional
Lean implication, not its physical premises or C0 membership.

The compiled declaration inventory is
`sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/nonvacuum_componentwise_car_slack_2026-10-26.json`
(SHA-256 `e8e47ed94880a0508a529f70ce0be3720dedb2fee1edb93e9a34bb3e7c0539d8`).
It queries public import `D0.All` with Lean 4.30.0 on dirty snapshot
`f3063366bd90156ec6286e2c924916c97ed99fae`; the complete Lean-source digest

### Vector-norm midpoint refinement on the existing CAR history (26 October 2026)

The exact source remains #334 commit
`fe5cf335da04cac14e50407b03f98b96be17dc52`, file blob
`67531a343a0ae707ff0a3830c0e2c9cf2bbc8213`, §§7.1–7.2, Eq. (24). The
previous Lean result established only a coordinatewise estimate and a separate
componentwise slack condition. This follow-up formalizes the vector-norm
refinement and its half-margin implication without replacing or renaming the
existing two-component recurrence.

In `D0.Analysis.MidpointQuadrature`,
`midpointQuadrature_error_norm_le_of_thirdDerivative_bound_on_interval` proves
the local vector defect bound `M * h^3 / 24` in any complete real normed space,
using the vector-valued integral Taylor remainder. It needs C³ regularity only
at points of that cell. The sampled-history theorem
`sampledVectorMidpointHistory_error_le_refinementBound_on_interval` composes
those defects to `duration * M * h^2 / 24` at every mesh point, with no
coordinate-count factor and C³ regularity only on the sampled interval. The
former theorem names remain global-smoothness compatibility wrappers.

In `D0.Geometry.NonvacuumBrinkmannStencil`, the Euclidean lift of `Fin 2 → ℝ`
preserves the existing squared matter norm. Lean proves that coordinate
projection commutes with derivatives, that vector samples and midpoint rates
are the lifts of the existing component samples and rates, and by induction
that the complete Euclidean recurrence equals `finiteMatterMidpointHistory`
pointwise after lifting. Therefore
`sampledMatterMidpointCARComplement_positive_of_vectorMargin_on_interval` gives
a vector-norm CAR-complement result on that same finite record.
`sampledMatterMidpointCARComplement_positive_for_all_sufficiently_fine_meshes_on_interval`
derives the eventual uniform-mesh version. The former global theorem names
remain compatibility wrappers. If `A` bounds the continuum Euclidean norm,

generator. It does not prove physical source selection, the original 40 varied

criterion remains available as a separate sufficient condition.

### Coefficient-bounded cubic matter profiles in the nonvacuum midpoint route (27 October 2026)

The source's arbitrary smooth two-component matter class now has a concrete
finite-dimensional application in the existing midpoint/CAR module. The
Euclidean lift of `cubicMatterProfile` is an actual cubic vector profile on
`[0,1]`. The sum of the four coefficient-vector norms bounds the profile
uniformly there. If this sum is strictly below `sqrt(1-p)`, the pointwise CAR
chart condition follows; if `c1 ≠ 0`, the theorem also proves a nonzero matter
rate at the initial point. The sampled-rate history is proved equal to the
existing recursively defined cubic matter record. The composed theorem then
returns both the CAR-complement bound and the exact five-point stencil source
for that same record, with one `N₀ > 0` valid at every site for every uniform
mesh `h=1/N`, `N ≥ N₀`.

the positive CAR complement, the exact five-point source expressed through

## Claim assumption links in the architecture view (27 October 2026)

The read-only `relation_view` now follows the existing `claims.csv.assumption_ids`
list to assumption rows by `assumption_id`. It reports each link's resolution
status and the assumption row's owner claim ID. The existing
`registered_assumptions` field remains the separate inverse relation selected
by `assumptions.csv.claim_id`; unresolved identifiers remain visible instead
of disappearing. No registry field or authority changed.

For `D0-LEAN-BRIDGE-001`, all six listed IDs resolve. Their owner claim IDs are
`D0-PHI-HURWITZ-001`, `D0-RG-001`, `D0-CARRIER-003`, `D0-SMOOTH-001`,
`D0-SMOOTH-001`, and `D0-LEAN-BRIDGE-001`, respectively. The owner relation
therefore still returns only `ASSUMP-HST-EXTERNAL` for this claim. The view now
shows both existing relations without merging ownership into claim use or
creating application edges.

This is a registry crosswalk only. The IDs do not become Lean propositions,
the theorem does not gain assumption binders, and the physical bridge remains
open. A synthetic test covers mixed comma/semicolon separators and missing IDs;
a repository regression checks all six real links and their distinct owners.

## Finite archive sub-Gaussian source and retired external shells (27 October 2026)

`D0.Probability.SubgaussianArchive` now embeds each rational finite archive
measure as a Lean `PMF`, defines real coordinate and linear-projection random
variables, and states sub-Gaussianity using Mathlib's actual
`HasSubgaussianMGF`. A centered bounded projection is proved sub-Gaussian by
Mathlib's Hoeffding lemma. For the configured eight-atom cross measure, every
projection has support in the explicit interval with radius
`(Σ k, |u k|)/5`; its covariance quadratic is exactly
`(1/100) * Σ k, u k^2`, hence satisfies the registered `1/4` slack. The
finite PMF support, centering, full-dimensional support, covariance and MGF
facts are assembled by `archive_stage_finite_hst_source_facts` in
`D0.Probability.FiniteArchiveHSTInput`. Full dimensionality is stated as a
positive-mass atom outside every hyperplane through the origin. The source
package contains only internal finite-measure facts; it is not the external
comparison theorem's complete input record.

This proves properties of the configured fixed finite distribution: it does
not construct a refining probability family or map it to the BOOK archive
operator `P_archive = -δ₀⁸/30 Π_N`. Nor does it prove convex-order domination
by a Gaussian or an entropy-selected coupling. The cited source's softmax
conclusion has convex-order and strict-slack hypotheses; see [Hua, Song, and
Tudose, *On Talagrand's Convexity Conjecture*, Proposition 3.3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10908).
The former `HSTExternalInterface`, `EntropyCouplingKernel`, and
`ArchiveClassicalGaussianChannel` declarations were proposition shells
carrying caller-supplied conclusions; those source files and public imports
have been removed. Historical candidate-resolution snapshots retain their
original locators. The entropy and channel claims are now `OPEN` under
`ASSUMP-HST-EXTERNAL`.

The registry maps `D0-HST-ARCHIVE-001` only to the finite-source theorem,
records its explicit module support in `formal_support.csv`, and maps
`D0-ARCHIVE-ENTROPY-001` and `D0-ARCHIVE-GAUSSIAN-CHANNEL-001` to open proof
targets. This is a scoped repair of formal meaning and claim status, not an
external HST proof, a BOOK transport, or acceptance of C0.

The focused modules passed (`D0.Probability.SubgaussianArchive`, 3,527 Lake
jobs; `D0.Probability.FiniteArchiveHSTInput`, 3,528 jobs), and the final
public-import build passed for `D0.All` (4,741 jobs, Lean 4.30.0). The
elaborated declaration inventory confirms the public theorem type
`∀ n, D0.FiniteArchiveHSTSourceFacts n`, its source range, dependencies and
axiom closure `[Classical.choice, Quot.sound, propext]`. The inventory was
collected at snapshot `dc5da7cad8a545f40fd8318fb44cdbb3fe4037e1` with
`worktree_dirty=true`; its whole-Lean-source digest is
`3c949d1896d18aa51af7d809d7543cf7bb2cfdad4d2ff850979ad0f53992ef36`, claim
registry digest `84e38794e87ff52377424c0a0f4d8d59c0fdcbab2fad17bf24c656170c76baee`,
and collector digest
`2daef4969b731f89870e4b85530ba92c89113a4767cefcfc950c9ae278a876a6`. The
JSON is
`sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/finite_archive_hst_source_inventory_2026-10-27.json`,
SHA-256 `49d203f5b66f731c9648c0131b62c9e2699b00991de1280f333e4c228ad365d0`.

Generated Lean views and claim-map coverage pass; the formalization-debt

## Scalar stationarity requires a neutral source (27 October 2026)

The exact-owner review exposed an additional scope mismatch in the `BOOK_07`
§07.32 scalar reduction. The Lean theorem
`D0.scalar_stationarity_implies_archive_poisson` in
`03_FORMALIZATION/D0/Interface/ScalarPoissonReduction.lean` has the explicit
type

```text
ScalarSectorStationary n φ ρ →
NeutralSource ρ →
ArchivePoissonEquation φ ρ
```

This premise is structural: `ScalarSectorStationary` tests only zero-sum
perturbations, which imply equality of all Poisson residual entries but do not
set their common value to zero. Neutrality makes the residual sum zero and
therefore forces that constant to vanish. The additional compiled declaration
`D0.scalar_stationarity_without_neutrality_counterexample` witnesses the
failure at `n = 0`, `φ = 0`, `ρ = 1`: stationarity holds, while source
neutrality and the Poisson equation both fail.

Accordingly, the claim row now names the exact owning module and both the
conditional theorem and counterexample; `formal_support.csv` exposes that
module. The prior `D0.Active.ScalarPoissonReduction` path remains a one-line
compatibility import of that canonical owner and is explicitly retained in the
support closure; it is not listed as the theorem's owner. `BOOK_07` §07.32 and
its theorem-chain table now state the neutral-source premise and mark the
unconditional version false. This preserves the proved conditional finite
result and removes the stronger unsupported wording. It does not change the
physical GR route or its open source-selection obligations.

The changed scalar module passes its narrow build; the generated `ClaimMap`
passes its two-job narrow build; and final `D0.All` passes all 4,741 jobs.
The 72 focused inventory/view/coverage tests pass, as do generated-view
freshness, claim-map coverage, repository validation (zero warnings), work
validation, and the formalization-debt non-growth check (5 findings, matching
the 5-entry baseline). These checks validate the finite source correction;
they do not close the physical GR obligations above.

The two declarations were extracted from clean snapshot
`fb5e8eabb30138d7ba9624048c6f20fd341a38fc` with Lean 4.30.0 and public import
`D0.All`. The compiled inventory is
[`scalar_poisson_neutrality_declarations_2026-10-27.json`](sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/scalar_poisson_neutrality_declarations_2026-10-27.json),
SHA-256 `44c3d548c884e52045fafb2e1bae468c6161ae9779e52c8e5957622c9e36e160`.
Its whole-Lean-source digest is
`50f40d0fb6f37ead704b6d6808cf9b210058d234f3280bc86f9318f690f46c09`,
collector digest `4c00408d3095d509d158cb54e08b09a057ebba0b99e55e68fb5ed9b845dad408`,
and the scalar owner source SHA-256 is
`dbf2111d4a18f92c442f3cdeb9e97292bc9ed8b7835f8437293a7d2a00d10718`. The
matching `claims.csv` SHA-256 at that snapshot is
`5ff7828b5ee8da5257304ecf689bb56b527d6af226c4b01b469a3ec827fdc1b1`.
The compiled type records both required premises, and the counterexample

## Supported one-dimensional cochain refinement and source boundaries (27 October 2026)

The existing research result in
[`A4D_NATIVE_COCHAIN_REFINEMENT.md`](A4D_NATIVE_COCHAIN_REFINEMENT.md) and its
unchanged Lean capsule are the source for this migration. The capsule SHA-256
is `c07153c478ab7ebafd361107d848a45be47650d93fceaad2c5c67bd052f96515`.
The supported `Archive1DCochainRefinement` module now exposes its explicit
`Fin (m+1)` to `Fin (m+2)` vertex and edge maps, oriented cyclic incidence,
both counting Gram forms, unscaled chain identity and energy pullback. The
edge map's standard-basis matrix has `B₁ᵀ B₁ = I`, and a separate theorem
identifies that matrix's action with the edge map. The vertex matrix has
`B₀ᵀ B₀ = I + E₀₀`, with diagonal 2 at the repeated vertex and 1 elsewhere.
The module also carries the
one-dimensional degree correction `c = (m+2)/(m+1)` for scaled incidence.

The new `Archive1DCochainRefinementBinding` consumer proves that the vertex
map is the existing archive projection and `archiveLiftOperator` on each
`N+2` cycle, including equality of the actual lift matrix and its Gram. It
substitutes the existing `forwardDifferenceScale` into the
corrected one-dimensional block. At `N=0`, the first edge is an exact negative
control: the uncorrected fine and coarse scaled expressions are 3 and 2.
The research capsule is retained unchanged;
`python3 tools/verify_native_cochain_supported_transport.py` builds the
supported binding and checks definitional equality with the capsule's four
map/scale definitions under the shared build lock, with source digest and
axiom output. This is a finite mathematical application, not an admission of
the physical refinement or a typed four-dimensional `dForward` theorem.
Source-to-claim acceptance remains pending in the existing 1D claim.

The next typed substitution for the graded claim uses the already supported
`D0.archiveRolePhaseProjection` transported through
`archiveRolePhasePointGroupEquiv`. For a fine role coordinate `i` and coarse
size `L=N+2`, the decisive missing lemma is that projecting one fine
`roleTranslatePlus` step gives the coarse translated point when `i<L`, and
the unchanged coarse point on the collapsed edge `i=L`. This is a stated
proof target, not a compiled theorem here. It supplies the masked scalar
forward-difference application; the full graded composition additionally
needs the occupied-role mask and the existing `carCreate_support` and
`carCreate_degree_raise` maps. The fixed-torus readout obstruction in the
research source remains a separate downstream boundary.

The previously `LEAN_PROVED` graded-refinement row overstated its cited owner:
that owner proves role and label cardinalities and does not define `B_S` or a
graded chain equation. The row and module documentation now retain the 4D
statement as an open proof target; the research note's unscaled tensor
argument and its scale convention remain identified. In the Hodge arithmetic,
the full counting total at `L=2` is 625, whereas 256 is the total after the
degree-dependent partial normalization. Both numbers are recorded as scalar
arithmetic; the Lean file does not construct the full Gram matrices or prove
metric uniqueness. The cubical label counts similarly do not construct the
grid-indexed complex or its homology.

The edge-zeta owner proves a scalar identity for the defined expression. Its
Lean module does not construct `F_E` or equate its operator trace to that
expression; the deprecated trace target retains that open binding, while the
separate Puiseux target remains open. The floating-point diagonal certificate
is not promoted to an exact Lean trace adapter. These scope corrections await
the required independent source-to-claim acceptance and do not close C0 or
any physical GR premise.

### Historical pairing replay and current compatibility

The pairing-refinement certificate's 66-source ledger and its 12-input Lean

runner admits these inputs only for the named pairing checker, verifies the
unchanged checker and receipts plus every ledger input, restores the isolated
tree after execution, and rejects an unbound mismatch. It preserves the old
expected evidence rather than rewriting it for current files. The result is
historical source and receipt replay, not a current Lean build.

actual maintained imports and compiles the unchanged pairing Lean capsule,
recording its printed owner types, axioms, source digest and toolchain. This
checks compatibility with current sources; it does not establish native
admission or accept a scientific source-to-claim relation. The historical
checker and this current-source compilation are distinct checks.

### 27 October Perron/GNS and comparison-map registry reconciliation

A separate source-and-type review of the previously staged Perron/GNS scope
correction found two surviving contradictory claim descriptions. The
`D0-FIBONACCI-AF-TOWER-OWNER-001` note now describes the actual all-level
finite **real** matrix refinement, its specified Perron-weighted functional and
algebraic pairing. The bundled `fibonacci_af_tower_owner` proves initial
dimensions, scalar golden identities and the two-step incidence identity; the
more general finite maps and pairing are separate declarations. The named
`gns_refinement_isometry` is conditional on the supplied algebraic maps and
preservation hypotheses. No complex AF C*-limit, positive normalized state,
uniqueness, completed GNS Hilbert space, Hilbert isometry or Dixmier
normalization is established by these declarations. In particular the
specified level-zero functional has value `phi + 1` on its unit. The claim's
historical book/section locator still names the broader AF/GNS programme; it is
not the scope of the retained Lean proof.

`D0-VNEXT-DIRAC-LAPLACIAN-COMPATIBILITY-OWNER-001` now confines its NO-GO to
identification requiring equal finite dimensions: the existing theorem proves
`dimA N != 33` for every `N`, not that arbitrary or canonical `Xi_N` maps are
absent. `D0-VNEXT-FESHBACH-TOWER-COMPATIBILITY-OWNER-001` retains its open
transport/compatibility target without asserting a general obstruction. Its
linked historical checker sets `xi_exists = False` as a literal and prints a
stronger conclusion; it is retained as historical provenance, not evidence for
nonexistence. The separate historical UCP/interlacing scenario in BOOK_02 is
not a general no-map theorem. The existing claim IDs, Lean locators, lifecycle
and release statuses are preserved. An independent read-only review compared
the exact three-row change to the Lean owners and BOOK_02; this scoped review
is not independent acceptance of the C0 corpus, the AF/GNS target, or the
comparison map.

### 27 October counting Gram of the actual graded pullback

`D0.Geometry.ArchiveGradedCochainGram` now proves the diagonal counting Gram
bilinear form of `archiveGradedCochainPullback` against the existing
`cochainPairing`, for every level and all pairs of cochains. For a coarse
point `x` and Fock state `S` the diagonal weight is

```text
c_L^(2 * fockDegree S) * product_r
  (if S r is occupied then 1 else if x r = 0 then 2 else 1),
where L = N+2 and c_L = (L+1)/L.
```

The proof counts fibers of the actual role-group projection: a collapsed
fine edge contributes nothing in an occupied role, while an unoccupied
zero coordinate has two vertex preimages. It then pushes the real fine
pairing through that fiber count. Independent read-only review checked the
formula, full carrier, all quantifiers and source scope. The theorem has no
extra input hypothesis and its Lean axiom report contains `propext`,
`Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; it does not depend on the CAR
`native_decide` lemmas used by the chain-map theorem.

This is a bilinear Gram identity, not yet a separate matrix or trace theorem.
In particular 625 is the source's **unscaled** counting total at `L=2`;
256 is a different partially normalized sum. The expected counting total
for the actual degree-scaled map is `50625/16`, but that numerical trace has
not been separately compiled here. A unique physical Hodge metric,
normalization, readout and refinement remain open. The existing Hodge claim
retains `OPEN / PROOF-TARGET`; the historical source and its certificate
hashes are unchanged.

`D0.All` passed locally with 4,746 jobs after registering this module, on Lean
source digest `3e1413743001a2af50d6dfa2c6f3643be43f8854bcefcc13c97d91053657b9cf`.
The older `hodge_mass_trace_sum` theorem-name locator remains unresolved; it
is not made valid by the new Gram theorem.

On clean commit `4385d4244a85d8ae0cd1551e9c4e5f5d863a4562`, the elaborated
Gram, actual weighted fiber and chain declarations were recorded in
[`archive_graded_cochain_gram_declarations_2026-10-27_4385d424.json`](sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/archive_graded_cochain_gram_declarations_2026-10-27_4385d424.json)
(SHA-256 `74c78231f9a35c61088efe4884e265988935fd4e3b4562711417be671016a65f`).
The current locator replay found 2,581 atoms in declared modules, four
unresolved atoms, and 178 rows without theorem locators; this is not C0

### 27 October actual graded Gram matrix and its counting trace

The next supported consumer, `ArchiveGradedCochainGramTrace`, constructs the
matrix `archiveGradedPullbackMatrix` from images of the actual basis cochains
and proves its `mulVec` is the existing graded pullback. It applies the
bilinear Gram theorem to those basis images to prove that `BᵀB` is the
specified diagonal matrix. Summing the actual diagonal gives the general
counting trace `((L+1) + L*c_L²)^4`; the level-zero result `50625/16` is a
corollary of that general theorem. These theorems supersede only the preceding
checkpoint's statement that the matrix and trace had not yet been compiled.
They do not prove a separate fixed-sector trace, the partially normalized
quantity 256, a unique physical Hodge metric or physical realization.
Independent read-only scope review checked the actual operator binding and
normalization. The trace theorem's Lean axiom report is `propext`,
`Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`, without the CAR `native_decide`
dependencies of the chain theorem. The historical unresolved theorem-name
locator `hodge_mass_trace_sum` is retained and explicitly marked unresolved.

### 27 October counting trace for each actual Fock sector

`ArchiveGradedCochainSectorTrace` now takes the columns for any actual Fock
state `S` from `archiveGradedPullbackMatrix`, while retaining **all** fine
site × Fock rows. It proves the resulting sector Gram matrix is the diagonal
of the previously derived full weights. Its theorem
`archive_graded_sector_matrix_trace` establishes, for every `N` and every
`S`, the scaled counting trace

```text
c_L^(2k) * L^k * (L+1)^(4-k),
L = N+2, c_L = (L+1)/L, k = fockDegree S.
```

The proof counts the actual occupied and unoccupied roles and consumes the
existing per-coordinate sum; it does not assume the displayed expression.
A separate read-only review checked the actual matrix restriction, all fine
rows, quantifiers and scale. This supersedes the previous checkpoint's
statement that the fixed-sector trace was not yet formalized. The theorem's
reported axioms are `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.
This remains a counting calculation for the specified mathematical map, not a
selection of physical Hodge weights, a unique metric, refinement admission or
GR. The Hodge claim remains `OPEN / PROOF-TARGET`.

(SHA-256 `7631f8fa98155686a1f66ea6be02d979ab0e94b9e649275bf7756acc4d0a3579`).
The counts do not establish semantic claim acceptance or full C0 coverage.

### 27 October metric-measure application to the actual graded Gram

`ArchiveGradedMetricMeasureBinding` migrates the generic all-degree
metric-measure identities from the unchanged, pinned cochain research
capsule and applies them to the actual graded pullback. For every level,
coarse site and Fock state it constructs `m_r = 2` at coarse zero and `1`
otherwise, `mu = product_r m_r`, and occupied-direction conductances
`c_L² * mu / m_r`. Their positivity and nonzero denominators follow from the
constructed masses. `archive_graded_metric_measure_weight` proves that the
existing `hodgeMetricMeasureWeight` at these exact arguments is the actual
scaled Gram weight; `archive_graded_metric_measure_gram_diagonal` composes it
with the existing matrix theorem to identify the literal diagonal of `BᵀB`.
The generic result retains its nonzero-mass premise: the separately proved
one-role zero-mass counterexample demonstrates that it cannot be dropped.

Independent read-only review checked the source-to-adapter formula, scale,
nonzero hypotheses and registry bindings. The supported result is a concrete
finite counting identity; it does not select a unique or canonical Hodge
metric, physical normalization, native source or admitted refinement. The
existing claim remains `LEAN_PROVED / FORMALISM` at this scoped level and the
broader Hodge claim remains `OPEN / PROOF-TARGET`. The axiom report for the
new diagonal theorem and counterexample contains `propext`,
`Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, with no new axioms or native decision.

The final public `D0.All` build including this binding passed 4,749 jobs on
Lean source digest `a9b170f97d9088708b7366d16896ad77917ecc1ff62d1859d8548a64ca12c7ca`.

At clean commit `bb0ae145f0df6643fbbdbc6aacd4b97b8eeb9fdf`, the elaborated
binding, counterexample and sector-trace declarations are recorded in
[`archive_metric_measure_binding_declarations_2026-10-27_bb0ae145.json`](sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/archive_metric_measure_binding_declarations_2026-10-27_bb0ae145.json)
(SHA-256 `8eecb9d5b504a6bcca8ec500731227a95f32062d0e1ff39fc86d60b752bfae02`);
the live owner-aware claim reuse is
[`metric_measure_owner_reuse_2026-10-27_bb0ae145.json`](sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/metric_measure_owner_reuse_2026-10-27_bb0ae145.json)
(SHA-256 `512367e076128a1e9027882e52c2a7cef3616a5e0b8b2d97de25af46cab37b66`).
The complete claim-locator replay found 2,588 atoms in declared modules,
four unresolved atoms and 178 rows without theorem locators; none is a
coverage or physical-acceptance measure. At the same snapshot the cold-start
replay found all 169 required references in 24/24 cases and passed the impact
control; its receipt is
[proof_architecture_cold_start_summary_2026-10-27_metric_bb0ae145.json](https://github.com/gvakhrushev/d0_15/blob/30ffef0f5a4353874f8966a4b69d09e534f3c47d/02_REGISTRY/research/sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/reports/proof_architecture_cold_start_summary_2026-10-27_metric_bb0ae145.json)
(SHA-256 `45d06a847365b36a99127bb8d19a1f2725a5d2d59d7536a366c8cd2f99d624c9`).
The matching search comparison is
[proof_architecture_search_process_comparison_2026-10-27_metric_bb0ae145.json](https://github.com/gvakhrushev/d0_15/blob/30ffef0f5a4353874f8966a4b69d09e534f3c47d/02_REGISTRY/research/sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/reports/proof_architecture_search_process_comparison_2026-10-27_metric_bb0ae145.json)

### 28 October source intake: nine chat packages and the moving-cut source

The existing #334 source commit `eebcb5e467742df3e9cf7fa9702042d7294aa742`
is now consumed by the current architecture through
[`D0_CHAT_RESULTS_INTEGRATION_2026_10_28.md`](D0_CHAT_RESULTS_INTEGRATION_2026_10_28.md).
That map preserves the late root-source universality and selection limitations
with the positive constructions, and identifies the remaining same-execution
native source map, full covector, gain, norm and refinement application. The
original owner remains #334; the intake creates no accepted scientific claim
or new physical selector.

All 74 restored UTF-8 members are present in individually readable form with
the original upstream inventory and a read-only SHA-256 verifier. The
original moving-cut proof and finite checker are present at their published
paths. Historical receipts are kept separately from new independent replay.
Archive integrity, finite numerical controls and semantic source review have
separate scopes; the original nine ZIP archives themselves were not available
for a fresh byte/CRC verification. The user-supplied moving-cut source remains
conditional on its fixed-rank positive-Hilbert carrier and actual admitted
variation, and is not a completed native 42-component or GR source proof.

The current formalization slice is also wired into `formal_support.csv` and
the generated `D0.All` imports. `D0.Algebra.MovingProjectorTransport`
formalizes the noncommutative projector-tangent and cut-transport algebra
used by that source, while `D0.Geometry.ArchiveGradedRefinementComposedReadout`
extends the existing open graded-refinement claim with literal multi-step
composition and a fixed-component sampling control. These are supporting
theorems only; physical readout choice, source-to-claim acceptance and
physical refinement remain outside their conclusions.
The existing `proof_architecture.py search` now retrieves the published
moving-cut note and the integration map; its regression test asserts that the
Lean support row is still reported as a metadata locator and that no physical
assembly-owner relation is inferred. The composed refinement module is found
through its exact `formal_support` locator to the existing open chain-map
claim.

The current #334 head is `c4a92cb0f2326bf7ae2a8127c49f5b65ea71ce5a`, four
commits beyond the previously ingested `eebcb5e` source. Its new conditional
root-cut/refoliation classification is preserved under
[`d0_334_refoliation_delta_2026-10-28`](sources/d0_334_refoliation_delta_2026-10-28/README.md)
with upstream blob verification and a separate normal/optimized replay.
Within its declared canonical 3-metric/two-scalar class, a strong hypersurface
deformation algebra selects the Einstein-scalar kinetic structure; the actual
D0 symplectic form, native lapse generators and controlled same-execution
bracket limit remain unproved. No assembly slot, claim status, Lean theorem,
or physical selector is inferred from this conditional result.

### Moving-cut feedback calculus: reusable formal support

The source owned in #334 at `eebcb5e467742df3e9cf7fa9702042d7294aa742`
is now connected to reusable finite-matrix calculus. The independent
`D0.Analysis.MatrixDeterminantDerivative` module derives the determinant
row-replacement formula for an actual differentiable curve, Jacobi's
inverse-trace formula at an invertible matrix, and the principal complex
log-determinant derivative on the slit plane. It is consumed by
`D0.Algebra.MovingProjectorCalculus`, which derives the projector tangent
equation from local idempotence, differentiates the cut and return on the
same curve, and proves the relative `−log det` feedback source with the
actual resolvent. For real fixed `z`, Hermiticity makes the determinant real;
slit-plane membership at the evaluation point implies the local positive
chart used in the real logarithm.

The finite example uses a differentiable rank-one orthogonal-projector
curve and a fixed unitary reflection. It exhibits a nonzero return derivative,
the exact weighted trace `−48/17` and feedback derivative `−24/17` at
`z=1/2`. This is formal support for
the conditional moving-cut calculation, not physical source selection.
`D0.Algebra.MovingProjectorPositiveChart` proves the slit-plane premise from
an orthogonal projector, `U†U=1`, and `0≤z<1` by an exact Gram decomposition,
and applies the same-curve derivative. The thermal derivative, moving Gram,
variable β/z, native admitted
field variation, complete Info/work/controller price and GR composition
remain open. The original #334 owner and scientific claim statuses are
unchanged. The detailed application and its explicit interface are recorded
in [`D0_CHAT_RESULTS_INTEGRATION_2026_10_28.md`](D0_CHAT_RESULTS_INTEGRATION_2026_10_28.md).
An independent read-only mathematical review of calculus blob
`37305b34459ffa99189f91fbc3338c96562dc4fd` checked the signs, order of
factors, slit-plane argument and the same-curve example. Its scope does not
include compilation, physical admission or acceptance of C0.
The same independent review checked the Gram decomposition and conditional
application in positive-chart blob `90bb3051765733979f214ddc146e0ca02189b257`;
it likewise does not establish native execution or physical selection.

`python -X utf8 tools/lean_task_build.py final` completed `D0.All` (4,756
jobs; Lean 4.30.0) with dependency digest
`a9c8ceda6f52dd316f754d8c403f904416325f31740783f8e183ee0d235ec58e` and
environment digest `b157524d943853ad0a23855c68a20a441915f0609104a93e68d53b4ad3c1a004`.
The [selected declaration inventory](sources/d0_proof_architecture_formalization_2026-10-24/moving_cut_feedback_declarations_2026-10-28.json)

`52ec5f72d061452de590c28c13736a8208bd22a2`; its SHA-256 is

links above now name the published commit containing the bytes whose SHA-256
was recorded at each checkpoint. Those publication commits are distinct from
